# Inonda Ltd — 20 YouTube Video Scripts (English female narration + Greek subtitles)

Contents: Videos 1-20. Narration is English for a warm, trustworthy female voiceover; Greek subtitles (SRT-style) follow each section. Items in [SQUARE BRACKETS] must be filled with real details. Greek text should be reviewed by a native speaker before publishing.


Format note: lines starting with ">" are the spoken narration (this is what gets word-counted). It is written for a warm, calm, trustworthy female voiceover, with "we" meaning Inonda Ltd. Under each narrated section you will find the Greek subtitles (Ελληνικά) as an SRT-style block (index, timecode range, two lines of max 42 characters). Timecodes are in the video timeline and the indexes run continuously through each video, so each video's blocks can be pasted together into one .srt file (the timecodes already start at 00:00:00,000 for each video). Everything else is production notes. Items in [SQUARE BRACKETS] are placeholders for the owner to fill with real figures (price, duration, phone/WhatsApp). Section timestamps are recalculated from the narration length at about 140 words per minute, so re-time them after the voiceover is recorded.

---

# VIDEO 1

**Working title:** Full Bathroom Renovation in Cyprus: Old to New, Step by Step

**Greek title (Ελληνικός τίτλος):** Ανακαίνιση Μπάνιου στην Κύπρο: Από Παλιό σε Καινούργιο, Βήμα προς Βήμα

**Voice direction:** Warm, calm and trustworthy female voice. Unhurried, about 140 words per minute, with a friendly smile in the voice. Short pauses at every full stop. Slightly brighter on the hook and the call to action, and extra steady and reassuring on the waterproofing section.

**Narrated words:** 1012 | **Approx. spoken runtime:** 7:10 at about 140 wpm

**Thumbnail ideas**
1. Split screen: old stained bathroom on the left, finished bright bathroom on the right. Text: "OLD TO NEW".
2. Close-up of a hand holding a tile trowel next to an open wall showing pipes. Text: "WHAT'S BEHIND THE WALL?"
3. Presenter pointing at a shower floor with a red arrow. Text: "DON'T SKIP THIS STEP".

## Hook (0:00-0:25)
> This bathroom in Cyprus had cracked tiles, a damp ceiling, and a shower that never drained properly. Sound familiar? In the next few minutes, we will show you how we take a bathroom from old to new, step by step. And we will share the one step that nobody sees, the one homeowners regret skipping more than any other. Stay with us.

**On-screen text:** "Old bathroom, Cyprus" then "Step by step".
**B-roll:** Fast cuts of the damaged bathroom: cracked tile, damp stain, slow drain, then a quick flash of the finished result.

## Intro (0:25-1:05)
> We are Inonda Ltd. We repair, renovate and maintain homes and apartments across Cyprus, from small repairs to full bathrooms and kitchens, damp treatment, painting, roofing and waterproofing. Inonda is a new company, but the technician in charge of every job has up to 22 years of hands-on repair experience, and knows how Cyprus buildings cope with heat, humidity and sudden rain. Today is a full bathroom renovation, and we will keep it honest. We will tell you what each stage involves, what can go wrong, and where the real cost sits. If you are planning a bathroom, this will help you ask the right questions before anyone picks up a hammer.

**On-screen text:** "Inonda Ltd, Cyprus".
**B-roll:** The owner standing in the doorway of the old bathroom. Wide shot of the street or building exterior.

## Step 1: Survey and plan (1:05-2:00)
> Step one is the survey, and it happens before any demolition. We check the walls for damp. We check where the pipes run, test the drain, and look at the electrics. In Cyprus, many older bathrooms have old galvanised or poorly joined pipework. Sometimes there is a shower tray that has been leaking quietly for years. Then we agree the layout with you. Moving a toilet or a shower costs more than keeping it in place, so it is worth deciding early. If anything structural is involved, or the building has shared walls or shared drains, speak to the building owner or management committee first, and check whether any permit or approval is needed. We are always happy to explain, in plain language, what is likely to apply to your building.

**On-screen text:** "1. Survey and plan" / "Check: damp, pipes, drain, electrics".
**B-roll:** Moisture meter on a wall, torch on pipework, phone photos of problem areas, sketch of the layout on paper.

## Step 2: Strip-out (2:00-2:45)
> Step two is the strip-out. Out come the old tiles, the sanitaryware, and any rotten flooring. This is the stage where surprises appear. We sometimes find hidden leaks, old repairs, or walls that are not straight. That is normal. What matters is how it is handled. A good contractor tells you straight away and shows you photos, rather than quietly adding a charge later. That is how we work, because honest communication matters more to us than any single job. We also protect the rest of your home, because tile dust travels everywhere. Doors are covered, floors are protected, and waste is removed daily.

**On-screen text:** "2. Strip-out" / "Surprises are normal. Hiding them is not."
**B-roll:** Floor protection going down, tile removal, bags of rubble, close-up of an exposed old leak.

## Step 3: Plumbing and electrics (3:05-3:45)
> Step three is first fix, which means plumbing and electrics while the walls are open. New supply pipes, new waste lines, and a proper fall on the drain, so water actually leaves the room. Electrics in a bathroom need real care. Sockets, lights and extractor fans must be suitable for wet areas, and installed by someone qualified. Please do not let anyone convince you this is a place to save money. Then we test the pipes under pressure before closing anything up. If there is a leak, you want to find it now, not after the tiles are on.

**On-screen text:** "3. Plumbing and electrics" / "Pressure test before closing up".
**B-roll:** New pipes being fitted, pressure test gauge, electrician working on a wall, extractor fan being installed.

## Step 4: Waterproofing, the step nobody sees (3:45-4:35)
> Here is the step we promised. Waterproofing. After the walls and floor are levelled, we apply a waterproof membrane to the shower area and the floor, and seal the corners and joints with tape. Then we let it cure fully, following the manufacturer's instructions. You never see this layer once the tiles are on, and that is exactly why people skip it. When it fails, the water goes into the wall, or through to the neighbour below. The tiles still look fine. The damage does not. This is the most expensive shortcut in any bathroom. Waterproofing is part of our everyday work, in bathrooms, terraces and roofs, so we never treat it as an optional extra.

**On-screen text:** "4. Waterproofing" / "Invisible. Essential."
**B-roll:** Membrane being rolled on, corner tape detail, a water test on the shower floor, cured membrane close-up.

## Step 5: Tiling (4:35-5:15)
> Step five is tiling. Good tiling is mostly preparation. Flat surfaces, the correct adhesive for the tile type, even spacing, and a plan for where the cuts fall, so you do not end up with thin slivers by the door. Large format tiles look modern, but they need a very flat surface. Then comes the grout. In the corners, and wherever two surfaces meet, we use a flexible sealant, not grout. Rigid grout in a corner will crack. It is a small detail, and it matters a great deal. Details like this are what quality workmanship really means.

**On-screen text:** "5. Tiling" / "Flexible sealant in corners".
**B-roll:** Setting out tiles dry, spirit level on wall, adhesive trowel pattern, grouting, sealant bead.

## Step 6: Fixtures and finishing (5:15-5:50)
> Step six is the finish. Toilet, basin, shower, taps, mirror, lighting, and the final clean. We check every tap, flush every cistern, and run the shower for a while to confirm there are no drips and the drain keeps up. Then we do a walkthrough with you and make a snag list, so any small things are fixed before we leave. We want you to be happy with the room, not just to have finished the job.

**On-screen text:** "6. Fixtures and finish" / "Walkthrough + snag list".
**B-roll:** Fitting a basin, testing taps, shower running, the owner doing a walkthrough.

## YouTube description
Thinking about renovating a bathroom in Cyprus? In this video, Inonda Ltd walks you through a full bathroom renovation from old to new, step by step: survey and planning, strip-out, plumbing and electrics, waterproofing, tiling, fixtures and finishing. We explain what each stage involves, where homeowners usually get caught out, and why waterproofing is the step you cannot afford to skip. We also cover what to ask a contractor before work begins, and how to check that a quote is clear and complete.

Prices and timings vary with size, materials and what is found behind the walls. Typical guide: [ADD REAL PRICE, e.g. EUR X-Y] and [ADD REAL DURATION].

Inonda Ltd provides property repairs, renovations and maintenance in Cyprus, including bathrooms, kitchens, damp treatment, painting, roofing and waterproofing. Honest quotes, clear communication, quality workmanship and local Cyprus knowledge are how we work. Have a bathroom you would like us to look at? Send us a message through the channel or on WhatsApp: [ADD PHONE/WHATSAPP] and we will arrange a visit and a written quote.

Chapters: 0:00 Intro, 1:05 Survey and plan, 2:00 Strip-out, 3:05 Plumbing and electrics, 3:45 Waterproofing, 4:35 Tiling, 5:15 Fixtures and finishing, 5:50 Cost and time.

Subscribe for more practical property videos from Cyprus.

## Step 1: The walkthrough and the priority list (1:05-1:55)
> First, walk through every room with a notebook, and sort the problems into three groups. Group one: safety and structure. Damp, leaks, faulty wiring, dangerous balconies, anything a tenant could be hurt by. Group two: things a tenant will notice immediately, like a tired kitchen, a poor bathroom, bad lighting, and old windows that do not close properly. Group three: cosmetic extras. Fix group one first, always. Do group two next. Group three is where you can trim the budget. This is exactly how we walk a property with an owner, and it keeps the plan clear and the quote honest. A rental does not need designer finishes. It needs to be clean, dry, safe, and durable.

**On-screen text:** "1. Priority list" / "Safety, then tenant-visible, then cosmetic".

## Step 2: Damp, leaks and the hidden problems (1:55-2:45)
> Step two is dealing with damp, leaks and anything hidden. Painting over damp is the classic mistake. It looks fine for a few weeks, then the stain returns. You have to find the source first. It could be a roof or terrace leak, a plumbing fault, condensation from poor ventilation, or moisture rising in a wall. Each has a different fix. Once the source is solved, the wall can be treated, dried and repaired properly. Damp treatment is a core part of our work, and we always start with the cause, never the stain. Ask your contractor to explain the cause in plain language. If they cannot, get a second opinion.

**On-screen text:** "2. Fix the cause, not the stain".

## Step 3: Electrics, plumbing and cooling (3:05-3:50)
> Step three is the systems. Wiring in old apartments can be outdated, so have a qualified electrician inspect it and test it, and replace whatever is unsafe. Check the plumbing for slow drains, weak pressure and old taps. And think about comfort. Tenants in Cyprus care about summer heat and winter cold, so working air conditioning, decent insulation where possible, and windows and shutters that actually seal make a property more attractive. Do check what your building allows before installing outdoor units, because some buildings have rules. Local knowledge helps here, and it is something we bring to every property we look at.

**On-screen text:** "3. Systems: electrics, plumbing, comfort".

## Step 4: Floors, walls and the kitchen and bathroom (3:50-4:40)
> Step four is the surfaces. Walls get repaired and painted in light, neutral colours, which make rooms feel larger and are easy to touch up between tenants. Floors should be durable. Tiles or good quality hard flooring handle wear much better than something delicate. The kitchen and bathroom matter most. You do not always need a full rebuild. Sometimes new doors on the existing kitchen cabinets, a new worktop and new taps will transform a kitchen. In the bathroom, fix leaks, re-seal, re-grout, or replace the sanitaryware if it is beyond saving. Tenants forgive plain. They do not forgive grubby or broken. Quality workmanship is what keeps a rental looking good between tenants.

**On-screen text:** "4. Surfaces: neutral, durable, easy to clean".

## YouTube description
Own an older apartment in Cyprus and wondering how to make it rentable? In this video, Inonda Ltd walks you through a renovation from old to modern rental, room by room. We cover how to build a priority list, how to deal with damp and leaks properly instead of painting over them, why electrics and plumbing come before decoration, how to choose durable surfaces, and how to refresh a kitchen and bathroom without always rebuilding them.

We also talk honestly about what to spend on and what to leave alone when you are renovating for tenants rather than for yourself. Costs and timings vary widely with the condition and size of the property: light refresh [ADD REAL PRICE, e.g. EUR X-Y], fuller renovation [ADD REAL PRICE, e.g. EUR X-Y], typical duration [ADD REAL DURATION].

This video is general information and not legal or tax advice. Please check rental rules and obligations with a qualified professional.

Inonda Ltd offers property repairs, renovations and maintenance in Cyprus, including damp treatment, painting, kitchens, bathrooms, roofing and waterproofing. Honest quotes, clear communication, quality workmanship and local Cyprus knowledge are how we work. If you would like us to look at your apartment, message us through the channel or on WhatsApp: [ADD PHONE/WHATSAPP] and we will arrange a visit and a written quote.

Chapters: 0:00 Intro, 1:05 Priority list, 1:55 Damp and leaks, 3:05 Systems, 3:50 Surfaces, 4:40 Finishing, 5:10 Cost and time.

Subscribe for more practical property videos from Cyprus.

Το βίντεο περιέχει γενικές πληροφορίες και δεν αποτελεί νομική ή φορολογική συμβουλή. Παρακαλούμε ελέγξτε τους κανόνες και τις υποχρεώσεις ενοικίασης με εξειδικευμένο επαγγελματία.

Η Inonda Ltd προσφέρει επισκευές, ανακαινίσεις και συντήρηση ακινήτων στην Κύπρο, μεταξύ άλλων αντιμετώπιση υγρασίας, βαψίματα, κουζίνες, μπάνια, στέγες και στεγανοποιήσεις. Ειλικρινείς προσφορές, καθαρή επικοινωνία, ποιοτική δουλειά και γνώση της τοπικής αγοράς της Κύπρου είναι ο τρόπος που δουλεύουμε. ## Step 1: Decide what can stay (1:00-1:55)
> Step one is to look at what you already have. Kitchen cabinet boxes are often in better shape than the doors on the front. If the boxes are solid and straight, and the layout works for you, you can keep them. Check the hinges and runners. Check for water swelling under the sink. And check whether the drawers still close properly. Keeping the layout also saves a lot, because moving plumbing, gas and electrics is where kitchen budgets quietly grow. If the layout truly does not work, then it is a bigger project. But try the simple route first. When we visit a kitchen, this is always our starting point, because the best saving is the thing you do not have to replace.

**On-screen text:** "1. Keep the layout and sound cabinets".
**B-roll:** Hand checking a cabinet base, opening drawers, looking under the sink for swelling, measuring a wall.

## Step 2: Where to save (1:55-2:45)
> Step two is where to save. New doors and drawer fronts on existing cabinets can change the whole look. Painting or wrapping is cheaper again, if the surface is sound. New handles cost little, but change the feel completely. A new splashback, or even just re-sealing and cleaning the old one, makes a visible difference. Open shelving in one area can replace a wall unit. And a plain, light colour scheme looks clean and modern, and it will not date quickly. Be careful with very cheap materials that swell when wet. In a Cyprus summer, with humidity, heat and cooking, poor materials fail quickly. Saving should never mean cutting corners on quality.

**On-screen text:** "2. Where to save: doors, handles, paint, splashback".

## Step 3: Where to spend (3:05-3:50)
> Step three is where to spend, even on a budget. Spend on the worktop, because it takes daily abuse, and choose something suited to hot cooking, water and cleaning. Spend on the sink and taps, because leaks start there. Spend on proper hinges and runners, because cheap ones fail first. And spend on the plumbing and electrics if they are old. A beautiful new kitchen connected to a bad pipe or an unsafe socket is not a saving. Appliances are another place to be honest with yourself. Buy what you will actually use, and check the dimensions before ordering anything.

**On-screen text:** "3. Where to spend: worktop, sink and taps, hardware, services".

## Step 4: The order of work (3:50-4:35)
> Step four is the order of work, which keeps costs down and avoids rework. First, strip out what is going. Second, any plumbing and electrical changes. Third, wall repairs and painting. Fourth, flooring, if it is being replaced. Fifth, cabinets, doors and worktops. Sixth, splashback, taps and appliances. Last, the final sealing, cleaning and adjustments. Painting before the worktop goes in, for example, saves you protecting a brand new surface. Measure twice, order once, and confirm delivery dates before you start, because waiting for one missing part can hold up the whole job. Planning the order well is one of the simplest ways we help owners save money.

**On-screen text:** "4. Order of work saves money".

## Step 5: Quotes, scope and surprises (4:35-5:15)
> Step five is how to handle the money side. Ask for a written, itemised quote, so you can see what is included and what is not. That is exactly how we prepare ours: clear, itemised and written down. Ask what happens if something unexpected turns up, such as damp behind a unit or an old pipe. Keep a small contingency, for example [ADD REAL PERCENTAGE OR AMOUNT] of the total, for the surprises that do appear. And do not choose purely on the lowest price. A very low quote often leaves out something you will pay for later.

## YouTube description
Want a better kitchen in Cyprus without paying for a complete rebuild? In this video, Inonda Ltd explains how to renovate a kitchen on a budget, step by step. We cover how to decide what can stay, where to save money, where it is worth spending, the order of work that avoids rework, and how to read and compare quotes properly.

You will learn why cabinet boxes can often be kept while the doors change, why worktops, taps and hardware deserve a better budget, and why old plumbing and electrics should be checked before you install anything new. We also talk about contingency money and why the cheapest quote is not always the cheapest outcome.

Costs and timings vary with the size of the kitchen and the materials chosen: refresh [ADD REAL PRICE, e.g. EUR X-Y], fuller renovation [ADD REAL PRICE, e.g. EUR X-Y], typical duration [ADD REAL DURATION].

Inonda Ltd provides property repairs, renovations and maintenance in Cyprus, including kitchens, bathrooms, damp treatment, painting, roofing and waterproofing. Honest quotes, clear communication, quality workmanship and local Cyprus knowledge are how we work. If you would like us to look at your kitchen, send a message through the channel or on WhatsApp: [ADD PHONE/WHATSAPP] and we will arrange a visit and a written quote.

Chapters: 0:00 Intro, 1:00 What can stay, 1:55 Where to save, 3:05 Where to spend, 3:50 Order of work, 4:35 Quotes and surprises, 5:15 Cost and time.

## Step 1: Inspection and finding the source (1:00-1:55)
> Step one is the inspection, and it is detective work. Water travels. It can enter at one point and appear on a ceiling metres away. So we look at the inside and the outside together. On the roof or terrace we check for cracks, blistering, lifted edges, damaged joints around drains, gaps where walls meet the floor, and any spot where water sits instead of running away. We check the drains and outlets for blockages. We check parapet walls, pipes and vents that pass through the surface, because those are common weak points. Sometimes a water test helps, where we isolate an area and wet it systematically to see where the leak appears. And we take photos before anything is touched, so you can see exactly what we see.

**On-screen text:** "1. Inspection: water travels".
**B-roll:** Moisture meter on the ceiling, walking the terrace, cracks near a drain, pooling water, a hose test on an isolated area.

## Step 2: Diagnosis and choosing the repair (1:55-2:40)
> Step two is the diagnosis. Not every leak needs a whole new roof. A single crack or failed joint might need a local repair. A tired, widespread surface might need a new waterproofing system across the whole area. Drainage problems need drainage fixes, otherwise the new surface will fail too. Ask the contractor to show you the problem and explain the reason in plain words. If the answer is just, we will put something over it, ask more questions. A good repair addresses the cause. We would always rather explain honestly and recommend the right fix than sell you a bigger job than you need.

**On-screen text:** "2. Diagnosis: local repair or full system?"
**B-roll:** The owner pointing at the defect, a drawing of water flow, a blocked outlet being cleared.

## Step 3: Preparation of the surface (2:55-3:40)
> Step three is preparation, and this is where most repairs are won or lost. The old surface is cleaned. Loose, blistered or failed material is removed. Cracks are opened up and repaired. Weak areas are made good, and the surface is levelled where needed, so water runs towards the drains instead of sitting in puddles. Then it must be completely dry. Applying a waterproof coating over a damp surface traps moisture and leads to blisters and failure. This is not the exciting part, but it is the part that decides how long the repair lasts. It is also the part we never rush.

**On-screen text:** "3. Preparation: clean, repair, level, dry".
**B-roll:** Pressure washing, scraping old coating, crack repair, mortar levelling, drying out a surface.

## Step 4: Waterproofing system (3:40-4:30)
> Step four is the waterproofing itself. Depending on the surface, this might be a liquid-applied membrane, a bitumen or similar sheet membrane, or another system suited to the building. Whatever is chosen, detail is everything. Corners, edges, drain outlets, pipes and the junction between the floor and the walls need extra attention, usually with reinforcement. The membrane is typically carried up the walls to a suitable height and finished properly. Several coats or layers are applied according to the manufacturer's instructions, with the correct drying time between each. Ask which product is used, and check that it is suitable for sun exposure, or that it will be protected by a finishing layer.

**On-screen text:** "4. Waterproofing: details decide success".
**B-roll:** Primer applied, reinforcement at corners, membrane rolled or brushed on, detail around a drain outlet and a pipe.

## Step 5: Protection, finishing and the water test (4:30-5:10)
> Step five is protection and testing. On a terrace, the membrane may be covered with screed and tiles. On a roof, it may get a reflective or protective coat to reduce heat and ageing. Then comes the part we like most. We test it. We block the outlets, flood the area to a safe depth for a period, and check the ceiling below. If it stays dry, we know the repair works. Then we clear the water, unblock the drains and finish the edges. Please ask your contractor whether they do a water test. It is the proof.

**On-screen text:** "5. Protect, finish, water test".
**B-roll:** Screed and tiles laid on a terrace, protective coat on a roof, flood test with a measured depth, dry ceiling check below.

## YouTube description
Has a stain appeared on your ceiling, or does your terrace leak when it rains? In this video, Inonda Ltd explains the full process for repairing a leaking roof or terrace in Cyprus. We cover how to find the real source of a leak, how to decide between a local repair and a full waterproofing system, why surface preparation decides how long the repair lasts, how the membrane and its details are installed, and why a proper water test is the proof that the job is done.

We also share simple prevention tips, including keeping drains clear and checking your surface once a year. Costs and timings vary with the area, access and condition: local repair [ADD REAL PRICE, e.g. EUR X-Y], full system [ADD REAL PRICE, e.g. EUR X-Y], typical duration [ADD REAL DURATION].

Inonda Ltd provides property repairs, renovations and maintenance in Cyprus, including roofing, terraces, waterproofing, damp treatment, painting, bathrooms and kitchens. Honest quotes, clear communication, quality workmanship and local Cyprus knowledge are how we work. If you have a leak you would like us to look at, send a message through the channel or on WhatsApp: [ADD PHONE/WHATSAPP], include a few photos, and we will arrange a visit and a written quote.

Chapters: 0:00 Intro, 1:00 Inspection, 1:55 Diagnosis, 2:55 Preparation, 3:40 Waterproofing, 4:30 Protection and water test, 5:10 Cost and prevention.

Subscribe for more practical property videos from Cyprus.

## Section 2: Why Cyprus houses get damp (0:50-1:40)
On-screen text: "Concrete frame + brick infill + little insulation = cold walls". Simple diagram of warm air meeting a cold wall.
B-roll: Exterior shots of typical Cyprus houses and apartment blocks; interior shot of a cold north-facing corner; steam from a kettle or shower; clothes drying indoors.

> Why is this so common here? Most Cyprus homes have a reinforced concrete frame with brick or block walls, often with little or no insulation and older windows. In summer, that is fine. In winter, the inside surface of those walls gets cold, especially on north-facing sides and in corners. At the same time, we cook, we shower, we dry washing indoors, and some of us use portable gas heaters, which release a lot of moisture. Warm, damp air touches a cold wall and turns into water. That is condensation, one of the most common causes of winter mould here. Local knowledge matters, because a coastal home and a hill-village home do not behave the same way.

## Section 3: Three kinds of damp and how to tell them apart (1:40-2:35)
On-screen text: "1. Condensation 2. Penetrating damp 3. Rising damp". Show each with a one-line description as it is spoken.
B-roll: Close-ups of each type: fine black spotting; a stained patch with a tide-mark edge on a ceiling or wall; flaking plaster low on an older stone wall with white salt deposits. Use your own footage, with permission from the property owner.

> Here is a useful clue. If the mould follows a straight line, like the edge of a ceiling or the shape of a column, you are probably looking at a thermal bridge. Concrete columns and beams stay colder than the brick around them, so moisture collects there first. Condensation is usually fine black or grey spotting. It wipes off, and it gets worse on cold, still days. Penetrating damp is different. It is a patch, often with a darker edge, and it gets worse after rain. And rising damp, mostly a problem in older stone houses, shows as staining and flaking plaster low on the wall, sometimes with white, powdery salts. If you are not sure which one you have, that is completely normal. Telling them apart is exactly what we do on a site visit.

## Section 4: Permanent fixes for condensation (2:55-3:50)
On-screen text: "Moisture. Air. Temperature." Then a numbered list of fixes.
B-roll: Extractor fan venting outside; a window opened briefly; a dehumidifier running; wardrobe pulled slightly away from a wall; external insulation being installed (if you have footage) or an illustration.

> Fixing condensation comes down to three things: moisture, air and temperature. First, reduce moisture at the source. Use extractor fans in the bathroom and kitchen that vent outside, put lids on pans, and dry washing in a ventilated space. Second, ventilate. Open the windows fully for ten to fifteen minutes each day, even in winter, rather than leaving them barely open for hours. Third, keep temperatures steady. A dehumidifier and gentle, constant heating usually work better than short bursts of strong heat. For mild cases, that may be enough. For persistent cases, the lasting answer is a warmer wall surface, with insulation outside the building or carefully chosen insulation inside. Which one suits your home is a job for a proper survey, and we are happy to carry one out.

## Section 5: Fixing penetrating and rising damp (3:50-4:35)
On-screen text: "Find the water. Stop the water. Then repair." Checklist: render cracks, window sealant, gutters, terrace, pipes.
B-roll: Cracked render; failed sealant around a window frame; blocked downpipe; technician using a moisture meter; breathable lime plaster on an old stone wall (if available).

> For penetrating damp, find the water entry and stop it before you touch the wall finish. Common causes are cracked render, worn window and door sealant, blocked gutters and downpipes, a leaking terrace, and pipes leaking inside walls. Once the source is repaired, let the wall dry properly, which can take weeks. Only then do we replaster and repaint. That order matters. Rising damp needs care. It is often misdiagnosed, so get a proper assessment before paying for any injection or special plaster. Older stone houses usually need breathable materials, such as lime-based plaster, because a hard waterproof coating can trap moisture and push the problem elsewhere.

## Section 6: Cleaning mould and mistakes to avoid (4:35-5:10)
On-screen text: "Do not: paint over damp. Do not: rely on anti-mould paint alone. Do not: mix cleaning products." Safety icons for gloves and mask.
B-roll: Gloved hand with a mould remover and cloth; peeling paint over a damp patch; a red cross overlay.

> Now, the mould itself. Clean it with a suitable mould remover, wear gloves and a mask, open the windows, and never mix cleaning products. Then deal with the cause, or it will come back. The most common mistakes are painting over damp, which simply peels, and treating anti-mould paint as a cure, when it is only a surface finish. Large areas of mould, or anyone at home with breathing difficulties, are reasons to get advice quickly.

## YouTube description (English)
Damp walls and mould in Cyprus homes are one of the most common winter problems, and the wrong fix usually means the mould comes straight back. In this video we explain why Cyprus houses get damp in winter, how to tell condensation, penetrating damp and rising damp apart, and what the permanent fixes look like.

We cover thermal bridges in concrete-frame buildings, ventilation and moisture habits, when insulation is worth considering, why you should find the water source before repainting, and how to clean mould safely. We also explain when a specialist survey is the sensible next step. This video is general guidance only; every property is different, and structural or persistent damp problems should be assessed on site.

Chapters:
0:00 Why mould keeps coming back
0:15 Who we are
0:50 Why Cyprus houses get damp
1:40 Three kinds of damp
2:55 Fixing condensation
3:50 Penetrating and rising damp
4:35 Cleaning mould and mistakes to avoid
5:10 When to call a specialist

Need help with damp or mould in your home? Inonda Ltd takes care of damp treatment, repairs, renovation, maintenance and painting in Cyprus, with honest quotes and clear communication. Message us on [ADD PHONE/WHATSAPP], or send us a message through this channel. Send a few photos and tell us the area and the property type.

Next video: How to prepare your roof and terrace for the Cyprus rainy season. Subscribe so you do not miss it.

Inonda Ltd | Property repairs, renovations and maintenance in Cyprus | Facebook and Instagram: @InondaLTD | Website: inonda.site

## Περιγραφή YouTube (Ελληνικά)
Η υγρασία και η μούχλα στα σπίτια της Κύπρου είναι από τα συχνότερα χειμερινά προβλήματα, και η λάθος λύση σημαίνει συνήθως ότι η μούχλα επιστρέφει αμέσως. Σε αυτό το βίντεο εξηγούμε γιατί τα σπίτια στην Κύπρο αποκτούν υγρασία τον χειμώνα, πώς ξεχωρίζετε τη συμπύκνωση, την υγρασία από διείσδυση και την ανερχόμενη υγρασία, και πώς μοιάζουν οι μόνιμες λύσεις.

Καλύπτουμε τις θερμογέφυρες σε κτίρια με σκελετό από σκυρόδεμα, τον αερισμό και τις συνήθειες που παράγουν υγρασία, πότε αξίζει να σκεφτείτε μόνωση, γιατί πρέπει να βρείτε την πηγή του νερού πριν ξαναβάψετε και πώς να καθαρίσετε τη μούχλα με ασφάλεια. Εξηγούμε επίσης πότε η αυτοψία ειδικού είναι το λογικό επόμενο βήμα. Το βίντεο περιέχει μόνο γενικές οδηγίες. Κάθε ακίνητο είναι διαφορετικό, και τα δομικά ή επίμονα προβλήματα υγρασίας πρέπει να ελέγχονται επιτόπου.

After a long dry summer, roofs and terraces have been baked by the sun for months. Joints open up, and dust and leaves collect in the drains. Then the first heavy rain tests everything at once. The good news is that dry weather is the best time to inspect and repair, because most waterproofing needs dry surfaces to work properly.

## Section 2: Safety first (0:50-1:15)
On-screen text: "Safety: no wet roofs, no edges, no rotten ladders. When in doubt, call someone."
B-roll: A stable ladder being secured; a parapet; pointing out the edge.

> First, safety. Never go on a roof in wet or windy conditions. Keep away from unprotected edges, never step on skylights or fragile sheets, and use a stable ladder with someone holding it. If your roof is steep, very high, or in poor condition, ask a professional to inspect it. A leak is cheaper than a fall.

## Section 3: The checklist, part one: water paths (1:15-2:20)
On-screen text: "1. Drains and outlets 2. Gutters and downpipes 3. Falls and ponding". Ticks appear as each item is spoken.
B-roll: Close-up of a clogged drain outlet; clearing leaves by hand with gloves; water poured from a bucket to test flow; a downpipe running to the ground; a puddle that stays after watering.

> Number one is drainage, because water that cannot leave will find another way in. Clear every roof drain, outlet and scupper of leaves, dust and debris. Check gutters and downpipes for blockages, loose brackets and cracks. Then pour a bucket of water across the surface and watch where it goes. It should flow towards the drains. If you see ponding, meaning puddles that stay for a day or more, the surface falls may not be right, and standing water ages waterproofing faster. A small puddle is not a disaster, but make a note and talk to a professional, because correcting the slope is usually a bigger job than patching. We are always honest about this: if a patch will do, we will say so.

## Section 4: The checklist, part two: the surface and joints (2:20-3:20)
On-screen text: "4. Waterproofing membrane 5. Edges, parapets, upstands 6. Penetrations 7. Tile joints and sealant".
B-roll: Blistered or cracked membrane close-up; corner where roof meets parapet wall with a gap; base of a solar water heater or aerial; cracked tile grout; sealant gun and fresh sealant bead.

> Number two is the waterproof layer. Look for cracks, blisters, splits, or areas where the coating is flaking or has gone chalky. Pay special attention to where leaks usually begin: the corner where the roof meets a parapet wall, the base of solar water heaters, tank stands and aerials, pipes that pass through the roof, and any expansion joints. Then check your terrace tiles. Missing grout, loose tiles and cracked sealant let water in underneath, where you cannot see it. Inside the house, look at ceilings and the tops of walls for stains, bubbling paint or a musty smell. And take photos now, so you can compare later. Clear photos also help us give you an accurate quote.

## Section 5: Repair options, from small to large (3:40-4:50)
On-screen text: "Small: clean + reseal | Medium: patch repair | Large: full re-waterproofing". Budget placeholders on screen: "[ADD REAL PRICE, e.g. EUR X-Y]" for each tier.
B-roll: A technician applying sealant; a patch repair with reinforcement; a membrane or liquid waterproofing being applied; a finished terrace.

> There are three levels of repair. Small repairs include cleaning the drains, resealing around pipes, and replacing cracked sealant or grout. Medium repairs mean patching a damaged area of waterproofing. That must be done properly, with the surface clean, dry and primed according to the product instructions, and with overlaps at the edges. Large repairs mean re-waterproofing the whole roof or terrace, sometimes after fixing the falls or replacing damaged screed and tiles. Which one you need depends on the age and condition of the existing system, and that needs a site visit. Please be careful with quick fixes. Painting over a leak, or rolling a thin coat over damp or loose material, usually fails, often just when the rain arrives. Costs depend on area, access and system, so [ADD REAL PRICE, e.g. EUR X-Y per square metre] and [ADD REAL DURATION, e.g. X-Y days] are placeholders until we have seen your roof and given you a clear, honest quote.

## Section 6: Apartment blocks and what to do if it is already leaking (4:50-5:25)
On-screen text: "Shared roof? Tell the management committee." "Already leaking: protect, photograph, call."
B-roll: A communal roof; bucket under a drip; photo being taken of a ceiling stain.

> If you live in an apartment block, the roof is often shared, so speak to your management committee early. If water is already coming in, move furniture, protect electrics, put a bucket down, photograph the damage, and have the source found. Do not assume the leak is directly above the stain, because water can travel along slabs and pipes before it appears. Tracing the real source is where local experience really helps.

## YouTube description (English)
Get your roof and terrace ready for the Cyprus rainy season with this practical checklist. In this video we explain why leaks usually start in dry weather, how to inspect drains, gutters, falls, waterproofing, parapet joints, penetrations and terrace tiles, and what repairs look like from a simple reseal to full re-waterproofing.

We also cover safety on roofs, what to do if water is already coming in, and why apartment block roofs should be raised with your management committee. Waterproofing materials and methods vary, and the right repair depends on the age and condition of the existing system, so a site assessment is recommended for anything beyond simple cleaning and resealing. This video is general guidance and not a substitute for an inspection.

Chapters:
0:00 Why leaks start before the storm
0:15 Why do this now
0:50 Safety first
1:15 Checklist: drains, gutters and falls
2:20 Checklist: waterproofing, joints and tiles
3:40 Repair options
4:50 Apartment blocks and active leaks
5:25 Next steps

Need your roof or terrace checked? Inonda Ltd takes care of roofing and waterproofing, repairs, renovation and maintenance in Cyprus, with honest quotes and clear communication. Message us on [ADD PHONE/WHATSAPP] or contact us through this channel. Please send photos and your location in Cyprus.

Next video: Salt air and sea-view damage, and what to repair first on coastal Cyprus properties. Subscribe to see it.

Inonda Ltd | Property repairs, renovations and maintenance in Cyprus | Facebook and Instagram: @InondaLTD | Website: inonda.site

## Περιγραφή YouTube (Ελληνικά)
Ετοιμάστε το δώμα και την ταράτσα σας για την περίοδο βροχών στην Κύπρο με αυτή την πρακτική λίστα ελέγχου. Σε αυτό το βίντεο εξηγούμε γιατί οι διαρροές ξεκινούν συνήθως σε ξηρό καιρό, πώς να ελέγξετε τις αποχετεύσεις, τις υδρορροές, τις κλίσεις, τη στεγανοποίηση, τους αρμούς στο στηθαίο, τις διελεύσεις σωλήνων και τα πλακάκια της ταράτσας, και πώς μοιάζουν οι επισκευές, από μια απλή επανασφράγιση μέχρι πλήρη νέα στεγανοποίηση.

Καλύπτουμε επίσης την ασφάλεια πάνω στη στέγη, τι να κάνετε αν το νερό μπαίνει ήδη, και γιατί οι στέγες πολυκατοικιών πρέπει να συζητούνται με τον διαχειριστή ή την επιτροπή. Τα υλικά και οι μέθοδοι στεγανοποίησης διαφέρουν, και η σωστή επισκευή εξαρτάται από την ηλικία και την κατάσταση του υπάρχοντος συστήματος, γι' αυτό συνιστάται αυτοψία για οτιδήποτε πέρα από τον απλό καθαρισμό και την επανασφράγιση. Το βίντεο περιέχει γενικές οδηγίες και δεν αντικαθιστά μια επιτόπια επιθεώρηση.

Κεφάλαια:
0:00 Γιατί οι διαρροές ξεκινούν πριν από την καταιγίδα
0:15 Γιατί να το κάνετε τώρα
0:50 Πρώτα η ασφάλεια
1:15 Λίστα ελέγχου: αποχετεύσεις, υδρορροές και κλίσεις
2:20 Λίστα ελέγχου: στεγανοποίηση, αρμοί και πλακάκια
3:40 Επιλογές επισκευής

## Section 1: Why salt is a problem (0:15-0:55)
On-screen text: "Salt + moisture = faster corrosion". Simple diagram of a sea breeze carrying salt onto a house.
B-roll: Waves and shoreline; salt crust on a window; wide shots of coastal homes; the Inonda owner on a coastal street or balcony (voiceover continues).

> Welcome to the Inonda channel. We are a property repair, renovation and maintenance company in Cyprus, and we know how the coast treats a house. Near the sea, the air carries tiny salt particles that land on walls, windows, railings and equipment. Salt attracts moisture, and that moisture speeds up corrosion of metal and can damage paint, sealants and concrete. The closer you are to the shore, and the more exposed you are to the wind, the faster this tends to happen. So the smart approach is to repair in the right order.

## Section 2: Priority one, safety and structure (0:55-2:05)
On-screen text: "PRIORITY 1: Safety and structure. Railings. Balcony concrete. Anything people lean on or stand under."
B-roll: Rusty railing base where it meets the concrete; balcony soffit with rust stains; concrete spalling with exposed steel; a hand tapping gently on concrete. Do not show anyone leaning on a damaged railing.

> Priority one is anything that affects safety. Start with balcony and stair railings. Check the base, where the post meets the concrete, because rust often hides there. If a railing moves, wobbles or has corroded through, treat it as urgent and keep people away from it. Next, look at balcony and terrace concrete, especially the underside. Rust-brown stains, cracks following a line, or pieces of concrete falling away to expose steel bars, all mean the steel inside is corroding. Rust takes up more space than the original steel, so it pushes the concrete apart, and the damage speeds up. This is not a painting job. The loose concrete must be removed, the steel treated or replaced, and the concrete repaired properly. If you see exposed or swollen steel, ask a structural engineer or an experienced specialist to assess it before any work begins.

## Section 3: Priority two, keeping water out (2:05-2:55)
On-screen text: "PRIORITY 2: Water ingress. Window and door seals. Roof and terrace waterproofing. Cracked render."
B-roll: Cracked sealant around an aluminium window; salt-crusted door threshold; cracked render with rust bleed; terrace edge.

> Priority two is keeping water out. Salt can make sealants harden and crack faster, and worn seals around windows, doors and terrace edges let wind-driven rain into the walls. Cracked render does the same. Once water is inside, it feeds damp and corrosion behind the surface, where you cannot see it. So check the sealants around frames, door thresholds and terrace edges, and look for render cracks. Repair them before the rainy season. If your roof or terrace waterproofing is old, have it inspected too. This is everyday work for us: sealing, render repair and waterproofing, done carefully.

## Section 4: Priority three, metalwork, fixings and equipment (3:15-4:20)
On-screen text: "PRIORITY 3: Metal and equipment. Aluminium frames. Gates and railings. Air conditioner units. Solar heaters. Outdoor electrics."
B-roll: Pitted aluminium frame with white powdery corrosion; rusty gate hinges; air conditioner outdoor unit with corroded fins; a solar water heater frame; outdoor socket or light fitting with corrosion.

> Priority three is metalwork and equipment. Aluminium frames can show white, powdery corrosion and pitting, especially if the coating is damaged. Light surface corrosion can be cleaned and protected, but if a frame is deeply pitted or the hardware fails, replacement may be better value. Iron gates and railings should have rust removed back to sound metal, then be primed and painted with a system suitable for marine conditions. When replacing fixings, ask for stainless steel suited to coastal use, and avoid mixing different metals where they touch. Outdoor air conditioner units and solar heaters suffer too, so ask the installer about coastal protection and cleaning. And if you see corrosion on electrical parts, have a qualified electrician look at it.

## Section 5: Priority four, coatings and the routine that saves money (4:20-5:10)
On-screen text: "PRIORITY 4: Protection and appearance. Exterior paint. Timber. Regular rinse with fresh water."
B-roll: A painter preparing a surface; a hose rinsing a window and railing; a wooden pergola with peeling varnish; a before and after of a repainted railing.

> Priority four is coatings and appearance. Fresh paint is not just cosmetic. It is a protective layer, but it only lasts if the surface is prepared properly. Painting over loose rust or salt-covered surfaces fails quickly. So wash the salt off with fresh water, remove loose material, and use products designed for exterior and coastal conditions. Timber, such as pergolas and shutters, needs a good exterior treatment and regular care. And here is the cheapest habit of all. Rinse windows, railings and outdoor equipment with fresh water from time to time, gently, not with a high-pressure jet that could damage seals. Add a short inspection once or twice a year, and small problems stay small.

## YouTube description (English)
Living by the sea in Cyprus is wonderful, but salt air can damage railings, concrete, windows, metalwork, paint and equipment. In this video we explain what to repair first on a coastal Cyprus property, in priority order: safety and structure, keeping water out, metalwork and equipment, then coatings and appearance.

We cover corroding balcony railings, concrete damage where steel bars are rusting, worn seals and cracked render, pitting on aluminium frames, gates, fixings, outdoor air conditioner units and solar heaters, and the simple habit of rinsing with fresh water. Structural concerns, such as exposed or swollen steel in concrete, should always be assessed by a qualified specialist, such as a structural engineer, before repairs start. This video is general guidance only, and every property is different.

Chapters:
0:00 Sea views and salt damage
0:15 Why salt is a problem
0:55 Priority 1: safety and structure
2:05 Priority 2: keeping water out
3:15 Priority 3: metalwork and equipment
4:20 Priority 4: coatings and routine care
5:10 Planning and next steps

Own a property near the coast? Inonda Ltd takes care of repairs, waterproofing, render and sealant work, painting, renovation and maintenance in Cyprus, with honest quotes and clear communication. Message us on [ADD PHONE/WHATSAPP], or contact us through this channel, and send photos of the problem areas along with your location.

Next video: Keeping a Cyprus house cool in summer. Subscribe to catch it.

Inonda Ltd | Property repairs, renovations and maintenance in Cyprus | Facebook and Instagram: @InondaLTD | Website: inonda.site

## Περιγραφή YouTube (Ελληνικά)
Η ζωή δίπλα στη θάλασσα στην Κύπρο είναι υπέροχη, αλλά η αλμύρα μπορεί να φθείρει κάγκελα, σκυρόδεμα, κουφώματα, μεταλλικά, χρώματα και εξοπλισμό. Σε αυτό το βίντεο εξηγούμε τι να επισκευάσετε πρώτα σε ένα παραθαλάσσιο ακίνητο στην Κύπρο, με σειρά προτεραιότητας: ασφάλεια και στατική επάρκεια, προστασία από το νερό, μεταλλικά και εξοπλισμός, και τέλος επικαλύψεις και εμφάνιση.

Καλύπτουμε τα διαβρωμένα κάγκελα μπαλκονιού, τις φθορές στο σκυρόδεμα όπου σκουριάζει ο οπλισμός, τις φθαρμένες σφραγίσεις και τον ραγισμένο σοβά, τα βαθουλώματα σε κουφώματα αλουμινίου, τις πόρτες, τις βίδες και στηρίξεις, τις εξωτερικές μονάδες κλιματιστικών και τους ηλιακούς θερμοσίφωνες, και την απλή συνήθεια του ξεπλύματος με γλυκό νερό. Τα στατικά προβλήματα, όπως γυμνός ή πρησμένος οπλισμός στο σκυρόδεμα, πρέπει πάντα να εκτιμώνται από πιστοποιημένο ειδικό, όπως ένας πολιτικός μηχανικός, πριν ξεκινήσουν οι επισκευές. Το βίντεο περιέχει μόνο γενικές οδηγίες και κάθε ακίνητο είναι διαφορετικό.

## Section 1: How heat gets in (0:15-0:55)
On-screen text: "Heat enters through: 1. Roof 2. Windows 3. Walls 4. Air leaks". Simple house diagram with arrows.
B-roll: The Inonda owner on camera (voiceover continues); sun on the roof and west wall; heat haze; an infrared thermal camera image if you have one (only if you own it, do not use stock thermal imagery as your own result).

> Welcome to the Inonda channel. We are a property repair, renovation and maintenance company in Cyprus. Before spending money, it helps to understand how a house heats up. Heat gets in through the roof, through windows, through walls, and through gaps. In a top-floor apartment or a single-storey home, the roof is in the sun for most of the day, so we look there first. South and west windows let in a lot of heat too. Walls and air leaks matter, but they usually come after the roof and windows.

## Section 2: Step one, the roof (0:55-1:55)
On-screen text: "STEP 1: Roof. Reflective coating. Insulation. Waterproofing in good condition."
B-roll: Flat roof with dark, sun-heated surface; reflective white or light coating being applied; insulation boards being laid; solar heater and tanks on the roof.

> Step one is the roof. There are two main options. The first is a reflective, light-coloured coating, which reduces how much heat the surface absorbs. It can help, but results vary by product, so ask for the data sheet and be wary of dramatic promises. The second is thermal insulation, which slows heat moving through the roof into the rooms below. On existing flat roofs, insulation is usually combined with waterproofing work, so the layers must be designed together, otherwise you risk trapping moisture or damaging the waterproofing. If your waterproofing is already failing, fix that first. In an apartment block, the roof is often shared, so you will need the agreement of the other owners. That is why we plan insulation and waterproofing together, after a site visit.

## Section 3: Step two, shading (1:55-2:50)
On-screen text: "STEP 2: Shade the glass from OUTSIDE. Shutters. Awnings. Pergolas. Deciduous trees."
B-roll: External roller shutters closing; an awning over a south-facing window; a pergola with climbing plant; a tree shading a west wall; interior curtains for contrast.

> Step two is shading, and the key word is outside. Once sunlight has passed through the glass, the heat is already in the room. Curtains and blinds inside help with glare, but they do much less for heat than shading outside. External shutters, awnings, pergolas and louvres on south and west windows make a real difference. Trees help too, especially deciduous ones that give shade in summer and let light through in winter. Think about the sun's path. East windows get morning sun, west windows get hot afternoon sun, and a south-facing window can be shaded with a fixed overhang, because the summer sun is high.

## Section 4: Step three, windows and doors (3:10-4:10)
On-screen text: "STEP 3: Windows. Double glazing. Thermal break frames. Low-emissivity or solar control glass. Seals."
B-roll: Old single-glazed window with a gap; modern double-glazed unit cross-section (sample piece if you have one); worn rubber seals; a tradesperson installing a window.

> Step three is windows and doors. Single glazing and old frames let heat in and cool air out. Modern double glazing helps, and frames with a thermal break reduce heat transfer through the metal. Ask about glass with a low-emissivity or solar control coating, especially for large windows facing west or south. But replacing windows is expensive, so do the cheaper things first. Check the seals, repair gaps around frames, and fit shading outside. Replace windows when they are failing, leaking, or when you are renovating anyway. Prices vary widely by size, glass and frame, so [ADD REAL PRICE, e.g. EUR X-Y per window] is a placeholder, and any quote should list the glass specification.

## Section 5: Step four, walls, air leaks and ventilation (4:10-4:55)
On-screen text: "STEP 4: Walls and gaps. External wall insulation. Seal leaks. Night ventilation. Ceiling fans."
B-roll: External wall insulation being fixed to a wall (if available); window open at night with a gentle breeze; a ceiling fan; a tradesperson sealing a gap.

> Step four covers walls, gaps and ventilation. Insulating the outside of walls can reduce heat gain, and it also helps with winter damp, as we covered in an earlier video. But it is a bigger job, best planned with a specialist. Day to day, ventilation is free. If nights are cooler, open windows in the evening and early morning to flush out heat, then close up and shade the windows during the hottest hours. Ceiling fans help people feel cooler using little power. And check whether any government energy efficiency schemes are currently available, because conditions change.

## Section 6: Air conditioning, what doesn't work, and end call-to-action (4:55-5:45)
On-screen text: "Air conditioning works best on a well-shaded, well-insulated house." "Cost and time: [ADD REAL PRICE, e.g. EUR X-Y] | [ADD REAL DURATION, e.g. X-Y days]". WhatsApp icon. End screen.
B-roll: Air conditioner indoor unit with a closed-window shot; presenter or owner to camera; end card.

> What about air conditioning? It works best when the house is already shaded and insulated, because then it has less work to do. Set sensible temperatures, keep doors and windows closed while it runs, and keep the filters clean. What tends not to work on its own: heavy curtains, or a wall paint that promises miracles. So our order is roof, outside shading, windows, then walls and ventilation. Costs depend on the size of your property and what you choose, so [ADD REAL PRICE, e.g. EUR X-Y] is a placeholder for a real, honest quote. For advice on your own home, message us on [ADD PHONE/WHATSAPP]. Please subscribe, and thank you for watching.

## YouTube description (English)
How do you keep a Cyprus house cool in summer without simply running the air conditioner all day? In this video we go through what actually works, in order: the roof (reflective coatings and insulation), shading windows from the outside, upgrading windows and seals, then walls, air leaks and night ventilation, and finally how to get the best from air conditioning.

We explain why external shading beats internal curtains, why roof insulation and waterproofing need to be planned together, what to ask for when comparing double glazing, and why you should be cautious about products that promise dramatic results. Every building is different, so for flat roof insulation, wall insulation or window replacement we recommend an on-site assessment. This video is general guidance only.

Chapters:
0:00 Why your house is so hot
0:15 How heat gets in
0:55 Step 1: the roof
1:55 Step 2: shading
3:10 Step 3: windows and doors
4:10 Step 4: walls, gaps and ventilation
4:55 Air conditioning and what does not work

Want advice on keeping your own home cooler? Inonda Ltd takes care of roofing and waterproofing, repairs, renovation and maintenance in Cyprus, with honest quotes and clear communication. Message us on [ADD PHONE/WHATSAPP], or contact us through this channel. Tell us the type of property, which way it faces, and send a few photos.

Coming soon: How to hire a contractor in Cyprus, quotes and red flags. Subscribe so you do not miss it.

Πώς κρατάτε δροσερό ένα σπίτι στην Κύπρο το καλοκαίρι χωρίς να έχετε το κλιματιστικό αναμμένο όλη μέρα; Σε αυτό το βίντεο εξηγούμε με σειρά τι δουλεύει πραγματικά: η στέγη (ανακλαστικές επιστρώσεις και μόνωση), η σκίαση των παραθύρων απ' έξω, η αναβάθμιση παραθύρων και σφραγίσεων, μετά οι τοίχοι, οι διαρροές αέρα και ο νυχτερινός αερισμός, και τέλος πώς να αξιοποιήσετε καλύτερα το κλιματιστικό.

Εξηγούμε γιατί η εξωτερική σκίαση υπερτερεί των εσωτερικών κουρτινών, γιατί η μόνωση και η στεγανοποίηση της στέγης πρέπει να σχεδιάζονται μαζί, τι να ζητήσετε όταν συγκρίνετε διπλά τζάμια και γιατί πρέπει να είστε επιφυλακτικοί με προϊόντα που υπόσχονται θεαματικά αποτελέσματα. Κάθε κτίριο είναι διαφορετικό, γι' αυτό για μόνωση δώματος, μόνωση τοίχων ή αντικατάσταση παραθύρων συνιστούμε αυτοψία στο χώρο. Το βίντεο περιέχει μόνο γενικές οδηγίες.

## 0:28-1:28 Introduction and honesty about prices

> Hello, and welcome. We are Inonda, a property repair and renovation company here in Cyprus. We look after homes through repairs, renovations and ongoing maintenance, from bathrooms and kitchens to damp treatment, waterproofing and painting. Our technician, with up to 22 years of repair experience, has learned one thing above all. People deserve straight answers. So, before we start, one honest note. We are not going to give you one magic number, because it would mislead you. Every bathroom is different, and anyone who quotes a fixed price without seeing the room is guessing. What we can do is explain the drivers, so that when quotes arrive, you understand what you are paying for, and why they differ. Any figures you see on screen are planning ranges only. The only number that counts is a written quote for your own bathroom.

On-screen text: Planning ranges only. Your quote is the real number.

## 1:28-2:38 Driver one: size, layout and whether the plumbing moves

> Driver number one is layout. Size matters, but not as much as you might think. A small bathroom with a new shower, toilet and basin in exactly the same positions as before is a very different job from a bathroom where you want the toilet moved to the other wall. The moment pipes and drainage have to move, you are paying for more plumbing, more breaking of floors and walls, and more making good afterwards. Changing a bath into a walk-in shower is another common request. It looks simple in photos, but the floor often needs to be reworked so that water drains properly. This is where local knowledge helps. We know how homes in Cyprus are typically built, and what is often waiting under the floor. So before you fall in love with a design, ask yourself one gentle question. Can everything stay where it is? If the answer is yes, you have just protected your budget.

On-screen text: Driver 1: Does the plumbing move? then: Same positions = lower cost. Moving pipes = higher cost.
B-roll: Time-lapse of old sanitaryware being removed. Close-up of waste pipes in a floor. Simple floor-plan sketch with arrows showing a toilet being moved.

## 2:38-3:50 Driver two: what is behind the walls

> Driver number two is the one nobody enjoys talking about, and that is what is hidden. Once the old tiles come off, we sometimes find tired old pipes, damp that has been quietly spreading, or a floor that was never properly waterproofed. In many Cyprus homes, especially older ones, bathrooms were done to a very different standard decades ago. None of that is visible on the day someone visits to quote. So a good contractor will tell you this honestly, and will explain how any extra work will be priced and agreed before it happens. That is how we like to work at Inonda. Clear communication, and no unexplained extras. Waterproofing and damp treatment are the areas where we would never cut corners. A leak behind a finished wall costs far more to fix than doing it right the first time. So when you compare quotes, ask each person one question. What happens if you find a problem once the walls are open?

On-screen text: Driver 2: What is behind the walls? then: Ask: how are surprises priced and approved?
B-roll: Footage of stripped walls and floor, damp patches, a moisture meter reading, waterproofing membrane being applied (shot on a real job, not stock).

## 4:12-5:18 Driver three: materials, fittings and finish

> Driver number three is the part everyone thinks of first. Materials and fittings. Tiles, sanitaryware, taps, shower screens, mirrors and lighting can range from simple and practical to very high-end, and that is where your taste sets the budget. But there are two details people often miss. First, large-format tiles and complex patterns take longer and need more skill to lay. So labour rises, even if the tile itself is not expensive. Second, cheap fittings can cost more over time, if spare parts are hard to find or they fail early. Our advice is to decide where you want to spend. Perhaps a quality shower mixer and a good screen, with simple tiles elsewhere. Write down what you want before you ask for quotes, so everyone is pricing the same thing. And if you are not sure, we are always happy to talk through your options with you.

On-screen text: Driver 3: Materials and finish then: Tile size and pattern change labour, too.
B-roll: Tile samples laid side by side, large-format tile being cut and laid, close-ups of taps and shower fittings, a handwritten spec list on a clipboard.

## 5:18-6:18 Driver four: the practical side, access, trades and timing

> Driver number four is practical. Think about access. An apartment on the fourth floor without a lift is harder work than a ground-floor house, because rubble and materials have to be carried out. Check with your building management about working hours and rules for shared areas. Then think about trades. A bathroom usually needs a plumber, an electrician, a tiler and sometimes a painter, and someone has to coordinate them. If nobody does, you get delays, and delays cost money. A good contractor takes that coordination off your shoulders, and that is the standard we hold ourselves to. Some works may also need permission or notification, so ask your contractor, and if you are unsure, speak to a Cyprus lawyer or the relevant authority. Finally, remember that rubble removal and clean-up should be written into the quote.

## 6:18-7:08 Putting a budget together

> So how do you build a budget? Start with a site visit, not a phone call. Ask for an itemised written quote. Then add a contingency, a sensible buffer for surprises, rather than hoping none will appear. For a simple like-for-like refresh, the range we typically see is [ADD REAL PRICE, e.g. EUR X-Y]. For a full renovation with moved plumbing and new waterproofing, it is [ADD REAL PRICE, e.g. EUR X-Y]. And a typical timeline is [ADD REAL DURATION, e.g. X-Y working days]. Treat those as starting points only. Your own bathroom, your own choices, and your own written quote decide the real number. We believe a good quote should be honest, itemised, and easy to understand, and that is what we aim to give every client.

## YouTube description
How much does it cost to renovate a bathroom in Cyprus? In this video we do not throw around a single number, because every bathroom is different. Instead we walk through the four things that really drive the price: layout and whether the plumbing moves, what is hidden behind the walls, materials and finish, and the practical side of access, trades and timing. You will also learn how to build a realistic budget, why a site visit matters more than a phone quote, and what to ask every contractor before you say yes.

Timestamps:
0:00 Why two bathrooms cost so differently
0:28 Honest note about prices
1:28 Driver 1: layout and plumbing
2:38 Driver 2: what is behind the walls
4:12 Driver 3: materials and finish
5:18 Driver 4: access, trades and timing
6:18 How to build a budget

Planning a bathroom renovation, or want a second opinion on a quote you have received? Send us a message on WhatsApp on [ADD PHONE/WHATSAPP] using the link in this description, or leave a comment below, and we will get back to you. Website: [ADD WEBSITE]. Inonda Ltd provides property repairs, renovations and maintenance in Cyprus, including bathrooms, kitchens, damp treatment, waterproofing and painting. This video is general information, not a quote, and not legal advice. For questions about contracts or consumer rights, please check with a Cyprus lawyer.

Subscribe for practical, honest property videos from Cyprus.

## 0:28-1:25 Introduction and the simple rule

> Hello, and welcome. We are Inonda, a property repair, renovation and maintenance company here in Cyprus. People ask us this question all the time, and the honest answer is that it depends. But it does not depend on luck. There are four simple questions that help you decide. How old is it? What exactly is wrong? How much would the repair cost compared with a replacement? And will the repair actually last? Repairs and maintenance are a big part of what we do, so we like to look at the problem first, and talk about replacing only when it truly makes sense for you. In this video we will use those four questions on three things we see constantly in Cyprus homes. Boilers and water heating, windows, and flooring.

On-screen text: 4 questions: Age. Fault. Cost. Lifespan.

## 1:25-2:48 Boilers and water heating

> Let us start with boilers and water heating. Many homes in Cyprus use a solar water heater with an electric element as backup, and others use gas or electric boilers. So the details vary, but the logic is the same. First, age. If the unit is old and has needed repeated repairs, each repair is buying you less time. Second, the fault. A failed thermostat or a simple part is usually a repair. A leaking tank or heavy internal corrosion usually means replacement, because the tank itself cannot be fixed economically. Third, cost. A rule of thumb many tradespeople use is that if the repair is getting close to a large share of the price of a new unit, replacement deserves serious thought. Fourth, safety. Never ignore gas smells, scorch marks or electrical problems. Switch it off and call a qualified person. In coastal areas, salt air can age metal parts faster, so ask the technician what they see on yours. And if your roof is part of the picture, for example where a solar water heater sits, we can also check the roof and waterproofing around it.

On-screen text: Boiler: Thermostat or part = often repair. Leaking tank or heavy corrosion = often replace. and: Gas smell? Switch off. Call a qualified person.
B-roll: Owner inspecting a boiler, close-up of a corroded fitting, a thermostat being tested, a solar water heater on a Cyprus roof.

## 3:05-4:22 Windows

> Windows. Here the questions are a little different. What is actually wrong? If the frame is solid and the problem is a worn seal, a stiff handle, a failed hinge or a misaligned sash, repair is often sensible. Condensation between two panes of double glazing usually means the seal has failed, and that unit typically needs replacing, though often not the whole frame. If the frame itself is warped, rotting, badly corroded or cracked, repairs tend to be short-lived. Then think about comfort. If your home is hot in summer, cold and damp in winter, and noisy, an old single-glazed window may be costing you in energy and comfort every month. A leaking window can also let water into the wall around it, which is where our damp treatment and painting work often begins. So ask for a quote for both options, repairing the existing windows and replacing them, so you can compare honestly. Replacement price will depend on size, material and glass type, so ask for it itemised, and ask about lead times and installation too.

On-screen text: Windows: Seal, handle, hinge = often repair. Failed double glazing or damaged frame = often replace. and: Ask for BOTH quotes.
B-roll: Hand checking a window seal, condensation between panes, a gap with daylight showing, a measured opening, a new window being fitted.

## 4:22-5:32 Flooring

> Now flooring. The big question here is whether the problem is a surface issue or a base issue. One or two cracked or loose tiles can often be replaced, as long as you can match them, and that is a tricky point with older tiles. If many tiles are cracking, sounding hollow, or lifting, that can point to a problem underneath, such as movement, damp, or poor adhesion. Replacing tiles on top just repeats the failure. Scratched or worn wooden or laminate floors may be sanded, refinished or patched, depending on the type, while swollen boards after water damage usually need replacing along with finding the leak. Our advice is simple. Fix the cause first, then decide on the floor. If you are unsure, ask your contractor to explain why they think it has failed, in plain language. At Inonda, that is exactly what we aim to do, because you should always understand what you are paying for.

On-screen text: Floors: Surface problem = often repair. Hollow, lifting, spreading cracks = check what's underneath. and: Fix the cause first.
B-roll: Tapping tiles to find hollow ones, lifting a tile to show the base, matching tile samples, sanding a wooden floor, a damp patch on a floor.

## 5:32-6:22 A simple decision checklist

> Let us finish with a quick checklist. Is it old and failing repeatedly? Lean towards replacing. Is the fault small, clear and cheap to fix on something otherwise sound? Lean towards repairing. Does the repair cost approach the price of new? Replace, or at least get a second opinion. Is there a safety risk? Stop, and call a qualified person. And always ask for the repair price and the replacement price in writing. For a typical repair we see [ADD REAL PRICE, e.g. EUR X-Y], and for a typical replacement [ADD REAL PRICE, e.g. EUR X-Y], but your own quote is what counts. We believe in honest quotes and clear communication, so you know exactly what is being done, and why.

On-screen text: Old + repeated faults = replace. Small, clear fault = repair. Safety risk = call a pro. and the placeholder lines.

## YouTube description
Repair or replace? If your boiler, windows or flooring are causing trouble, this video gives you a simple, honest way to decide in a Cyprus home. We use four questions, age, the exact fault, cost compared with replacement, and how long the repair will last, and apply them to water heating and boilers, windows and double glazing, and floor tiles and other flooring. You will learn which faults are often a straightforward repair, which signs usually point to replacement, and why you should always ask for both quotes in writing before you decide.

## 1:13-2:09 Mistake one: choosing on price alone

> Mistake number one is choosing on the lowest price alone. A very low quote can mean the contractor has missed things, or plans to cut corners, or intends to add extras later. It can also be perfectly fine, for example a smaller business with lower overheads. The point is not to avoid cheap quotes, but to understand them. Put your quotes side by side and ask why they differ. If one is far lower than the others, check what has been left out. Materials, waste removal, finishing, or a scope that is thinner than the others. At Inonda, we believe in honest quotes, so you can see exactly what is included and what is not. You are comparing like for like, or you are not comparing at all.

On-screen text: Mistake 1: Lowest price only and: Compare like for like.
B-roll: Three quotes laid on a table with highlighters, tapping a line item.

## 2:09-3:01 Mistake two: no site visit, and a vague scope

> Mistake number two is accepting a price without a site visit, and without a clear scope. If someone quotes over the phone, or from two photos, that is a guess. A proper visit means measurements, questions, and looking at the hidden risks like damp or old pipework. Local knowledge matters here, because homes in Cyprus can hide very different problems depending on their age and how they were built. Then insist the scope is written down. What will be done, what materials, what finish, and what is not included. A vague scope is where almost every dispute begins. If it is not written, it is not agreed, and then it becomes your word against theirs.

On-screen text: Mistake 2: No visit, no clear scope and: If it's not written, it's not agreed.
B-roll: Contractor measuring a room, taking photos of a damp patch, a scope list being written.

## 3:17-4:10 Mistake three: large upfront payments and no payment plan

> Mistake number three is paying too much upfront, with no clear payment plan. It is normal for a contractor to ask for a deposit to cover early costs, but how much is reasonable depends on the job. Rather than one big payment at the start, ask for payments linked to stages of work completed. Always pay in a way that leaves a record, and ask for receipts. If a contractor insists on a large cash payment before any work begins, that is a warning sign. This protects both sides, and it is how we like to keep things clear with our own clients. For the legal side of deposits and contracts, please check with a Cyprus lawyer before you sign.

On-screen text: Mistake 3: Big upfront payment and: Pay by stage. Keep records. Ask for receipts.
B-roll: A payment schedule table drawn on paper, bank transfer on a phone, a signed receipt.

## 4:10-5:00 Mistake four: not checking the contractor

> Mistake number four is not checking who you are hiring. Ask for photos of recent similar work, and ask if you can speak to a past customer. Ask whether they are a registered business, and about insurance. Ask who will actually be on site, because sometimes the person who quotes is not the person who does the work. Look at online reviews with a sensible eye, and remember that a few reviews prove very little either way. A good contractor will not be offended by any of these questions. If they are, that tells you something. We are always happy to answer them, and we think that is how trust should start.

On-screen text: Mistake 4: Not checking them and: Ask: past work, registration, insurance, who's on site?

## 5:00-5:53 Mistakes five and six: no timeline, and no plan for changes

> Mistake number five is having no timeline. Agree a start date and an expected duration in writing, and understand what might delay things, like waiting for materials. A typical duration for a job like yours might be [ADD REAL DURATION, e.g. X-Y days or weeks], but ask your own contractor. Mistake number six is having no plan for changes. Almost every renovation changes along the way. You find a problem, or you change your mind about a tile. That is fine, as long as extras are priced and agreed in writing before the work is done, not argued about afterwards. Clear communication is the thing we try hardest to get right, because most stress on a building project comes from surprises, not from the work itself.

On-screen text: Mistake 5: No timeline and Mistake 6: No change process then: Extras agreed in writing BEFORE the work.

## YouTube description
Hiring a contractor in Cyprus is one of the biggest decisions in any renovation, and a few simple mistakes can cost you months and a lot of money. In this video we walk through six common ones: choosing on price alone, accepting a quote with no site visit or written scope, paying too much upfront, not checking who you are hiring, having no timeline, and having no plan for changes and extras. For each one, you get a practical way to avoid it, and questions you can ask any contractor, including us.

Timestamps:
0:00 Why this matters
0:26 Introduction
1:13 Mistake 1: price alone
2:09 Mistake 2: no site visit or scope
3:17 Mistake 3: big upfront payment
4:10 Mistake 4: not checking the contractor
5:00 Mistakes 5 and 6: timeline and changes

Planning a repair or renovation and want a clear, written quote? Send us a message on WhatsApp on [ADD PHONE/WHATSAPP] using the link in this description, or ask in the comments, and we will get back to you. Website: [ADD WEBSITE]. Inonda Ltd provides property repairs, renovations and maintenance in Cyprus, including bathrooms, kitchens, damp treatment, waterproofing and painting. This video is general information only and is not legal advice. For contracts, deposits or consumer rights, please check with a Cyprus lawyer.

Subscribe for more practical property videos from Cyprus.

Σε αυτό το βίντεο εξετάζουμε έξι συνηθισμένα: επιλογή μόνο με βάση την τιμή, αποδοχή προσφοράς χωρίς επιτόπια επίσκεψη ή γραπτό εύρος εργασιών, υπερβολική προκαταβολή, έλλειψη ελέγχου του εργολάβου, απουσία χρονοδιαγράμματος και απουσία σχεδίου για αλλαγές και έξτρα. Πώς να διαβάσετε μια προσφορά ανακαίνισης στην Κύπρο: τι πρέπει να περιλαμβάνει και τα προειδοποιητικά σημάδια

## Thumbnail ideas
1. A quote document with a magnifying glass and red circles, with text: "READ THIS BEFORE YOU SIGN".
2. A red flag planted on a one-line quote reading "Renovation: one price", with text: "RED FLAG?".
3. Split image: a vague one-line quote with a cross, an itemised quote with a tick, and text: "GOOD vs BAD QUOTE".

## Hook (0:00-0:28)

> A renovation quote that fits on one line is not a quote. It is a guess with a space for a signature. Here is how to read a quote in Cyprus, what a good one includes, and the red flags to look for before you sign anything. Take a breath, make a coffee, and let us go through it together.

On-screen text: One line. One price. Red flag.
B-roll: A one-line quote on a clipboard, then the Inonda owner to camera holding an itemised quote.

## 0:28-1:19 Introduction

> Hello, and welcome. We are Inonda, a property repair, renovation and maintenance company in Cyprus. A quote is the document everything else depends on. If it is clear, the job usually goes smoothly. If it is vague, problems appear later. That is why we put so much care into ours, whether the work is a repair, a bathroom or kitchen renovation, damp treatment, waterproofing or painting. In this video we will show you what a good quote should contain, how to compare quotes properly, and the warning signs to watch for. Please note, this is general guidance, not legal advice. Before you sign any contract, it is worth having a Cyprus lawyer read it.

On-screen text: Not legal advice. Have a Cyprus lawyer read the contract.
B-roll: A quote document on a table, the owner pointing at the top section.

## 1:19-2:12 What a good quote includes: the basics

> Let us start with the basics. A good quote has the contractor's full business details, your name and the address of the property, and the date. It should say how long the quote is valid, because material prices can change. Then it should describe the work. Not just bathroom renovation, but what exactly. Removing old tiles, new waterproofing, new plumbing points, tiling, fitting sanitaryware, painting. Think of the description as a shopping list of tasks. If a job is on your wish list but not in the quote, assume it is not included until it is confirmed in writing. At Inonda, we write our quotes this way on purpose, because clear communication at the start saves a lot of worry later.

On-screen text: Basics: Business details. Your address. Date. Validity. and Description: tasks, not slogans.
B-roll: Highlighting each section of a sample quote; a checklist ticking itself.
